what does he mean by 1/10 index etf

**A:**

→ **$XSP** is the CBOE Mini-SPX index, it is literally sized at **1/10th** the value of the standard **$SPX** index.

→ **Benefit:** Lower premium costs and smaller contract sizes make it accessible for smaller accounts to trade index exposure without the high capital requirements of full-sized **$SPX** options.

→ **Structure:** Like **$SPX**, it is European-style and cash-settled, meaning no underlying shares are assigned at expiration.

**Asker:** Wock (The Oriental) (`sleep04025`) in #💬-stonks-yapping-💬

**Q:** [MESSAGE BEING REPLIED TO — from omniwiz — user_id 1422761344322502807]
"→ **$XSP** is the CBOE Mini-SPX index, it is literally sized at **1/10th** the value of the standard **$SPX** index.

→ **Benefit:** Lower premium costs and smaller contract sizes make it accessible for smaller accounts to trade index exposure without the high capital requirements of full-sized **$SPX** options.

→ **Structure:** Like **$SPX**, it is European-style and cash-settled, meaning no underlying shares are assigned at expiration."

[Wock (The Oriental)'s message to you]
What’s the catch?

**A:**

→ **Liquidity:** Volume is significantly lower than standard **$SPX**, expect wider bid-ask spreads that can eat your edge if you're scalping or trading size.

→ **Execution:** Higher slippage in fast markets, getting filled at your target price is harder when the order book is thin compared to the deep liquidity of the main index.

→ **Chain Depth:** Fewer strikes and expiries listed, you won't find the same granular, deep-out-of-the-money or short-term weekly variety available on **$SPX**.

→ **Best use:** It’s a sizing tool for smaller accounts, not a replacement for the liquidity of the primary index.

[YOU said earlier]: → **$XSP** is the CBOE Mini-SPX index, it is literally sized at **1/10th** the value of the standard **$SPX** index.

→ **Benefit:** Lower premium costs and smaller contract sizes make it accessible for smaller accounts to trade index exposure without the high capital requirements of full-sized **$SPX** options.

→ **Structure:** Like **$SPX**, it is European-style and cash-settled, meaning no underlying shares are assigned at expiration.

Wock (sleep04025): <@1422761344322502807> what does he mean by 1/10 index etf
[YOU said earlier]: → **$XSP** is the CBOE Mini-SPX index, it is literally sized at **1/10th** the value of the standard **$SPX** index.

→ **Benefit:** Lower premium costs and smaller contract sizes make it accessible for smaller accounts to trade index exposure without the high capital requirements of full-sized **$SPX** options.

→ **Structure:** Like **$SPX**, it is European-style and cash-settled, meaning no underlying shares are assigned at expiration.
Wock (sleep04025): What’s the catch?
Grand Nagus Yeezy (grandnagusyeezy): it also trades 24/5
[YOU said earlier]: → **Liquidity:** Volume is significantly lower than standard **$SPX**, expect wider bid-ask spreads that can eat your edge if you're scalping or trading size.

→ **Execution:** Higher slippage in fast markets, getting filled at your target price is harder when the order book is thin compared to the deep liquidity of the main index.

→ **Chain Depth:** Fewer strikes and expiries listed, you won't find the same granular, deep-out-of-the-money or short-term weekly variety available on **$SPX**.

→ **Best use:** It’s a sizing tool for smaller accounts, not a replacement for the liquidity of the pr
